- **Links:** verified SYS-04 (#5), DBT-06 (#29), SYS-25 (#62), OPS-09 (#63), SRC-09 (#57), SRC-10 (#65, still open by design) · **reopened OPS-01 (#9)** · new OPS-10 (#66), OPS-11 (#67), SRC-11 (#68), DBT-19 (#69) · `scripts/experiments/probe_p1_end_to_end.py` (reused, unchanged)

### 2026-09-17 — OPS-01, third fix pass: the battery-skip mode
- **Question / hypothesis:** the second QA re-audit found the registered task still had Task Scheduler's own `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=True`/`StopIfGoingOnBatteries=True` defaults, untouched by the earlier `-StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun` fix, on a machine that has a battery. Does the live task actually carry those defaults right now, and does flipping them stick?
- **Method:** `(Get-ScheduledTask -TaskName "NewsAggregator Crawler Health Check").Settings | Select-Object ... DisallowStartIfOnBatteries, StopIfGoingOnBatteries` before touching anything, then `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries -StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun -ExecutionTimeLimit (New-TimeSpan -Hours 2)` + `Set-ScheduledTask`, then re-read the same properties.
- **Result:** confirmed `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=True`/`StopIfGoingOnBatteries=True` live before the change (matching the QA finding exactly). After `Set-ScheduledTask`: `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False`, `StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False`, `StartWhenAvailable`/`WakeToRun`/`ExecutionTimeLimit` unchanged at `True`/`True`/`PT2H`.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** this closes the *configuration* half of the ticket only. Per the ticket's own revised Done-when, closing requires an *observed* unattended `health_check` batch (~22:30 UTC / 05:30 WIB, no manual trigger that morning) — first eligible window is 2026-09-18 05:30 WIB. Don't close OPS-01 on settings alone again; check `crawl_log` for that window first. Documented the battery flags in `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py`'s docstring and OPERATIONS.md's "Task settings" section (including the verification `Select-Object` line), plus a note in OPERATIONS.md flagging that no unattended run has happened yet.
- **Links:** OPS-01 (#9), left open pending the 09-18 observation · `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py` (docstring only) · `OPERATIONS.md`

### 2026-09-17 — DBT-17: repairing the 142 pending-embedding chunk rows
- **Question / hypothesis:** DBT-17 (#58, P2) left root cause unidentified: 142 `chunks` rows (26 WARTAEKONOMI sitemap_ids, dated 2026-06-01) exist with `embedding_id IS NULL`/`chunk_status='pending_embedding'`. Can the write path that created them be identified, and can they be repaired without a re-crawl?
- **Method:** walked every commit that has ever touched the chunk-write path -- the original `9b08107`, the SYS-17 fix `103980b`, the SYS-25 fix `7cba3bf` -- plus `scripts/backfill_chunks.py` (already ruled out in the ticket). Checked whether any of them could structurally produce an `embedding_id=NULL` row in `chunks`. Then wrote `scripts/backfill_pending_embeddings.py` to repair in place: fetch the 142 rows (their `text_content` is already intact, no re-chunk/re-crawl needed), back them up to JSON, embed via `ChunkFAISSDatasource.save()`, and `UPDATE ... WHERE chunk_id=:id` (not INSERT, to avoid duplicating the row). `--dry-run` first, then for real.
- **Result:**
  - **Root cause identified, code-side: none of the three historical versions of the write path can produce this shape.** The *original* `save_chunk_data` only converted a chunk to a SQL row `if c.embedding_id` was truthy -- an unembedded chunk was silently *dropped*, never inserted with NULL. The *current* `save_chunk_data`/`ChunkFAISSDatasource._embed_batch` always populates `embedding_id` (recovered or freshly assigned) before any SQL insert -- also structurally impossible. Conclusion: the 142 rows were almost certainly written by a one-off manual/REPL action during the 2026-06-01 WARTAEKONOMI backfill session (one day before the separate SYS-17 incident), not a reproducible pipeline bug. No code fix was needed to prevent recurrence.
  - **Residual design note (not fixed, not urgent):** SYS-17's SQL dedup (`get_existing_sitemap_level_pairs`) treats "a chunks row exists" as "fully saved," not checking `embedding_id IS NOT NULL` -- which is *why* two rounds of `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` (looks for *missing* rows only) never touched these 142. If this shape ever recurred, the pipeline still wouldn't self-heal it today. Left as a documented gap, not bundled into this fix (would require turning `save_chunks` into an upsert, a bigger change for a bug shape current code can't produce).
  - **Repair executed on `newsaggregator`:** dry-run confirmed exactly 142/26 WARTAEKONOMI before touching anything. Real run: backed up all 142 rows to `backups/dbt17_pending_embeddings_20260917T145239Z.json`, embedded all 142 (`updated=142 skipped=0`), and updated in place. Verified: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM chunks WHERE embedding_id IS NULL` -> `0`. No duplicates introduced (`chunks` row count for the 26 sitemaps stays exactly 142, before and after; a separate, pre-existing duplicate check against those 26 sitemap_ids returns empty -- the duplicates that do exist elsewhere are DBT-09 (#44), unrelated). **Live-verified past just the DB flag**: queried FAISS with one repaired chunk's own full text and got `sitemap_id=7008, semantic_score=1.0` as the #1 hit -- the vector is real and retrievable, not just a status update.
  - 4 new unit tests (`tests/unit/test_backfill_pending_embeddings.py`): dry-run never constructs `ChunkFAISSDatasource` or writes a backup; a real run backs up the pre-repair rows and issues an `UPDATE` keyed by `chunk_id` (never an insert); a chunk that somehow comes back from `.save()` without an `embedding_id` is skipped, not written as NULL. Full suite: 339 passed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** when a "why does this bad row exist" ticket's own code-history check comes back clean (every version of the write path structurally can't produce the shape), stop looking for a code bug and treat it as a one-off data event -- the fix is a data repair, not a guard. Also: `backfill_orphan_chunks.py`'s "missing row" query and a "row exists but embedding_id is NULL" query are two different repair shapes; don't assume one script's `NOT EXISTS` check covers both.
- **Links:** DBT-17 (#58) fixed · `scripts/backfill_pending_embeddings.py` (new) · `tests/unit/test_backfill_pending_embeddings.py` (new) · `backups/dbt17_pending_embeddings_20260917T145239Z.json` (gitignored) · DBT-09 (#44), still open, unrelated duplicates noticed in passing

### 2026-09-17 — refreshing OPS-04's store-consistency snapshot after DBT-17
- **Question / hypothesis:** OPS-04 (#52)'s divergence table is from 2026-09-16, before SYS-25's MEDIAINDONESIA repair, the 50-sitemap VIVA backfill test, and today's DBT-17 fix. What does it look like now, and is the "1,183 sitemap_ids in L1 but not L2" row (never drilled into) actually a new bug or a restated view of something already known?
- **Method:** wrote `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` -- reads both FAISS docstores directly via `pickle.load` (per OPS-04's own repro note, no embedding model needed) and set-diffs against Postgres for all 9 rows of OPS-04's table. Then, for the unexplained "1,183" row specifically, checked whether those sitemap_ids also lack an `articles` row, a `chunks` row of any kind, and which `sources` they belong to.
- **Result:**
  | check | 2026-09-16 | now |
  |---|---|---|
  | L1 FAISS, no `articles` row | 10,680 | 10,630 (-50, the VIVA backfill test) |
  | L2 FAISS, no `articles` row | 9,497 | 9,447 (-50, same) |
  | L1 FAISS, no `chunks` row | 1,335 | 1,183 |
  | L2 FAISS, no `chunks` row | 152 | **0** |
  | `articles` rows, no `chunks` row | 152 | **0** |
  | L1 but not L2 | 1,183 | 1,183 (unchanged) |
  | sitemap_ids with 2+ L1 vectors | 187 | 187 (unchanged -- DBT-09, untouched) |
  | `chunks` status `pending_embedding` | 142 | **0** (DBT-17) |
  | `chunks` sitemap_ids not in FAISS | 0 | 0 (SYS-17 holding) |

  **The "1,183 L1 but not L2" row is not three separate findings -- it's one population, seen from three angles.** `only_l1 - chunks_any` = 1,183 (exact match), `only_l1 - articles` = 1,183 (exact match), and `SELECT DISTINCT sources FROM sitemaps WHERE sitemap_id = ANY(only_l1)` = **only `MEDIAINDONESIA`**. So all three "L1 FAISS no chunks / L1 not L2 / (implicitly) no articles" rows in OPS-04's table describe the same 1,183 MEDIAINDONESIA sitemap_ids: an L1 vector exists and *nothing else does* -- no L2 vector, no `chunks` row, no `articles` row.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** DBT-17 + SYS-25 + the 50-sitemap VIVA test fully explain every row that moved; nothing moved by accident. The 1,183-MEDIAINDONESIA population is materially different from both DBT-17 (which had a full `chunks` row with real text_content to reuse) and the VIVA backlog (which has full `chunks` rows, just no `articles` row) -- these have *only* an L1 vector, so a DBT-17-style in-place repair doesn't apply; the L1 docstore's own `page_content` (the two-paragraph summary) is recoverable, but the full article text needed for L2/`articles` is not, without a re-crawl. Posted as a refresh to OPS-04 (#52) rather than filing a new ticket for the drill-down itself. DBT-09's 187 duplicate-L1-vector sitemaps are completely unaffected by anything fixed today -- still needs its own migration.
- **Follow-up the same session:** the MEDIAINDONESIA population turned out to need its own ticket after all, once its root cause was traced -- filed as **DBT-20 (#70)**. It's the pre-SYS-17 FAISS-before-SQL crash shape at ~8x WARTAEKONOMI's scale, compounded by two gaps: `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` starts its query `FROM articles`, so a sitemap with no `articles` row at all (not just missing chunks) is invisible to it; and `backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`'s classification ignores `chunks`/FAISS state entirely, so 582 of the 1,183 got marked `crawl_status='rejected_filter'` (terminal, never retried) purely from a headline-only re-check that disagrees with the direct proof (a real FAISS vector) that the original title+body filter passed. MEDIAINDONESIA has saved 0 articles since 2026-06-02 (96 total against 12,482 sitemaps) -- consistent with the source having gone largely dormant around the same incident.
- **Links:** OPS-04 (#52), refreshed, not fixed · `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` (new, reusable) · DBT-09 (#44), confirmed unchanged · **DBT-20 (#70), filed, not fixed**

### 2026-09-17 — DBT-09: deduping 1,035 duplicate chunks rows and their FAISS vectors
- **Question / hypothesis:** DBT-09 (#44, P2) found 1,035 duplicate `chunks` rows (same `sitemap_id`/`chunk_level`/`chunk_index`, different `chunk_id`/`embedding_id`) and no unique index. Its own fix proposal predates SYS-25's finer dedup key -- does the underlying `_embed_batch` bug still reproduce today, is a hard FK from `entity_mentions.chunk_id` a real deletion risk, and can the orphaned FAISS vectors actually be cleaned up (the ticket offered "accept the extra vectors until next rebuild" as a fallback)?
- **Method:** confirmed current counts (1,035 groups: 187 at chunk_level=1, 848 at chunk_level=2; VIVA/GRIDID/PIKIRANRAKYAT) and that no unique index exists yet. Checked `entity_mentions` for FK conflicts against any duplicate-group row. Checked `FAISS.delete()`'s signature in the installed `langchain_community` 0.3.24. Fixed `_embed_batch` (added a `seen_this_batch` set, keyed the same as the existing-in-FAISS check) so two chunks sharing a key *within one call* can't both be embedded. Added a `UniqueConstraint` to `ChunksTable` and `scripts/migrate_chunks_unique.sql`. Wrote `scripts/dedupe_chunks.py`: per duplicate group, keep the `entity_mentions`-referenced row if one exists (abort on 2+ referenced, unresolvable), else keep the lowest `chunk_id`; back up losers to JSON; delete each loser's FAISS vector via `FAISS.delete()` (L1 or L2 by level) before deleting the SQL row. `--dry-run` first, then for real, then the migration.
- **Result:**
  - **`entity_mentions.chunk_id` is a hard FK into `chunks`** (not mentioned in the original ticket) -- checked and confirmed **zero** conflicts today, but `dedupe_chunks.py` guards against it anyway (same posture as `migrate_articles_unique_sitemap.sql`/DBT-07 took for `articles`).
  - `FAISS.delete(ids)` is real and supported in the installed version -- didn't need the "accept extra vectors" fallback.
  - Dry-run matched the ticket exactly: 1,035/187/848. Real run: backed up all 1,035 losers to `backups/dbt09_dedupe_chunks_20260917T155714Z.json`, deleted 187 L1 + 848 L2 FAISS vectors, deleted 1,035 `chunks` rows (`102192 -> 101157`, exactly `-1035`). Migration applied clean (`chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq` now exists).
  - Verified: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (... GROUP BY sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index HAVING COUNT(*)>1) t` -> `0`. Re-ran `probe_store_consistency.py` (from the OPS-04 refresh above): "sitemap_ids with 2+ L1 vectors" `187 -> 0`, every other row unchanged as expected.
  - **Verified past the DB flag, the way DBT-17 was**: for a deduped sitemap (36616), directly confirmed the *surviving* `chunk_id`'s `embedding_id` is present in the `NEWS_L2` docstore with matching metadata and byte-identical `page_content` -- the kept row's vector wasn't accidentally the one removed. (A `search_l2` self-query for this same chunk did NOT rank itself #1 -- investigated and it's an unrelated, pre-existing property of VIVA's corpus having near-duplicate football stories, not a repair defect; the direct docstore check is the reliable proof here, same lesson as DBT-17's truncated-query false alarm.)
  - 7 new unit tests (`test_dedupe_chunks.py`: loser-selection incl. the FK-conflict abort, dry-run touches nothing, real run deletes the right row+vector+backs up) + 1 new `_embed_batch` test (`test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py`: a duplicate key within one batch is embedded once) + 1 new ORM test (`test_orm_models.py`: `ChunksTable` declares the constraint). Full suite: 347 passed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** always check for FKs pointing *into* the table you're about to dedup before writing a delete-the-loser migration -- `entity_mentions.chunk_id` wasn't in DBT-09's own evidence section and would have been a silent landmine. `FAISS.delete()` is safe to rely on in this LangChain version; don't default to "leave orphaned vectors for the next rebuild" without checking whether the API actually requires it. `_embed_batch`'s existing-in-FAISS dedup and within-batch dedup are two different bugs that both had to be fixed -- SYS-17/SYS-25 only ever addressed the first.
- **Links:** DBT-09 (#44) fixed · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/faiss/chunk_faiss_data_source.py` (`_embed_batch`) · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/dataflow/write/sqlalchemy/table.py` (`ChunksTable.__table_args__`) · `scripts/dedupe_chunks.py` (new), `scripts/migrate_chunks_unique.sql` (new), `scripts/migrate.py` (registered) · `tests/unit/test_dedupe_chunks.py` (new), `test_chunk_faiss_embedding_recovery.py`, `test_orm_models.py` · `backups/dbt09_dedupe_chunks_20260917T155714Z.json` (gitignored) · OPERATIONS.md, CLAUDE.md updated

### 2026-09-17 — QA audit (third pass) of the post-batch work: OPS-01, DBT-17, OPS-04, DBT-20, DBT-09
- **Question / hypothesis:** the five commits after the second re-audit (`77b1570` OPS-01, `8fd6802` DBT-17, `c09c1cf` OPS-04, `32f1942` DBT-20, `12ffccc` DBT-09) each claim verification on production. Do the claims hold when re-derived independently, do the two new destructive scripts leave the stores consistent, is the code fix in DBT-09 complete, and were *both* test suites actually run?
- **Method:** (1) `tests/unit` and `tests/integration` as-is on `dev`. (2) Own read-only probes against `newsaggregator` + `NEWS_L1`/`NEWS_L2` (`pickle` docstores + `faiss.read_index`): duplicate `(sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index)` keys per docstore, every `chunks.embedding_id` resolved in the right level's docstore, metadata agreement, `index_to_docstore_id` contiguity, orphan counts, `entity_mentions.chunk_id` dangling refs. (3) Vector↔text alignment: re-embedded 300 docstore texts per store (150 random + 150 targeted at DBT-09 survivors / DBT-17 repairs) and compared to `index.reconstruct(i)`. (4) DBT-20's population re-derived from scratch (status split, articles, eligibility under `load_all_sitemaps`' WHERE). (5) `Get-ScheduledTask` / `Get-ScheduledTaskInfo` / `Win32_Battery` for OPS-01. (6) `--dry-run` of `migrate.py`, `dedupe_chunks.py`, `backfill_pending_embeddings.py` for idempotence; inspected both backup JSONs. (7) Mutation: reverted DBT-09's `seen_this_batch` guard. (8) Code-path probe: `_embed_batch([c, c])` with `c`'s key already in the docstore, then the same through `save_chunk_data` on `newsaggregator_test` + temp FAISS.
- **Result:**
  - **Data repairs hold.** 0 duplicate groups, index present, `schema_migrations` row present, 0 `embedding_id IS NULL`, all 101,157 rows `embedded`. L2: 79,749 vectors = 79,749 rows, 1:1. L1: 22,591 vectors / 21,408 rows → 1,183 orphans = exactly DBT-20's set. 0 duplicate keys in either docstore, 0 unresolved `embedding_id`s, 0 metadata disagreements, 0 dangling `entity_mentions`. **Alignment: cosine(fresh, stored) min = 1.0000 on all 600 samples** — `FAISS.delete()` did not misalign anything; the dev's "self-query didn't rank #1" is a byte-identical text under another key. All three scripts are idempotent on re-run.
  - **DBT-20's numbers are exact** (1,183 / 601 pending / 582 rejected_filter / all MEDIAINDONESIA / 96 of 12,482). Sharpening: the 601 `pending` are *eligible now* (cooldown expired) — starved by queue position (11,561 pending, LIMIT 1000) and by MEDIAINDONESIA not being run, i.e. DBT-04, not status. Commented on #70.
  - **OPS-01 config is live and correct** (`DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False`, `StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False`, `StartWhenAvailable/WakeToRun=True`, `NextRunTime 2026-09-18 05:30`, battery present, child runs with `cwd=REPO_ROOT`). Correctly left open pending the observed run.
  - **DBT-09's code fix is half a fix.** `_embed_batch` guards `seen_this_batch` only in the `elif` (new chunk); the `if key in existing` branch appends both copies. Unit probe: two DTOs with key `(9,1,0)`. End-to-end on `newsaggregator_test` + temp FAISS: `psycopg2.errors.UniqueViolation: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq"` → the batch's `save_chunks` rolls back → in `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` that is `logger.error` + `continue`, so every sitemap in the batch loses its `chunks` and `articles` rows for the cooldown window, its fresh vectors are already on disk (orphaned until retry), and `crawl_log` says `completed`. Live batches are PK-distinct since SYS-07/DBT-07, so today's exposure is `specific_sitemaps` callers and repair scripts — which is exactly the path DBT-20's repair will take. → **SYS-26 (#71)**, P2.
  - **The integration gate was red when DBT-09 closed.** `migrate_chunks_unique.sql` was applied to `newsaggregator` only; `test_schema_migrations_has_a_row_for_every_known_migration` failed on `newsaggregator_test` naming the file. The ticket's "347 passed" is the unit suite. Applied the migration to the test DB during the audit → 72 passed / 9 xfailed. → **DBT-22 (#73)**.
  - **`dedupe_chunks.py`'s "backup of every deleted row" is six key columns** (`chunk_id, sitemap_id, chunk_level, chunk_index, embedding_id, referenced`) — no `text_content`; not restorable. FAISS is deleted before SQL and a re-run after a partial failure raises on the already-missing ids. The one run that happened was clean (verified above); the script is now the documented repair tool, so → **DBT-21 (#72)**, P3. DBT-17's backup, by contrast, carries full rows.
  - **DBT-17's "residual design note" was a known gap left in this log instead of a ticket** (`get_existing_sitemap_level_pairs` counts a NULL-`embedding_id` row as saved; `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` can't see it either). → **DBT-23 (#74)**, P3.
  - Mutation: reverting the DBT-09 guard fails exactly one test (`test_duplicate_key_within_same_batch_is_embedded_once`). Unit suite 347 passed as claimed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** the *data* side of this batch is the best-verified work in the log so far — every number re-derives, both stores are internally consistent and aligned. The *code* side shipped with a fix that covers one of two symmetric branches and a test that only exercises the branch that was fixed; write the test for the *other* branch of any `if/elif` dedup before calling it complete. And "full suite" must mean both suites — the integration gate that DBT-06 restored was red within twelve hours and nobody looked. Three traps added above. `scripts/experiments/probe_store_consistency.py` is sitemap-set-level; the two checks that found things here were per-`embedding_id` and per-vector — fold both into OPS-04's eventual tool (commented on #52).
- **Links:** verified DBT-17 (#58), OPS-04 (#52) refresh, DBT-20 (#70) filing, OPS-01 (#9) config · DBT-09 (#44) data verified, code fix incomplete · **new: SYS-26 (#71), DBT-21 (#72), DBT-22 (#73), DBT-23 (#74)** · test DB migrated to `migrate_chunks_unique.sql` (`newsaggregator_test` now at 12/12)

### 2026-09-18 — OPS-01: first unattended scheduled health-check run
- **Question / hypothesis:** OPS-01 (#9) was left open three times because every `health_check` batch so far had been a manual trigger. The operator received a Telegram ⚠️ at ~05:32 WIB this morning — is that the first genuinely unattended 05:30 run, and does every independent record (Task Scheduler, `crawl_log`, JSON report, message text, the 36 h gap check) agree?
- **Method:** `Get-ScheduledTask | Get-ScheduledTaskInfo` (`LastRunTime`, `LastTaskResult`, `NumberOfMissedRuns`) and `.Settings`/`.Principal`; `SELECT date_trunc('minute', started_at), MIN(started_at), MAX(finished_at), COUNT(*) FROM crawl_log WHERE task='health_check' GROUP BY 1` on `newsaggregator`, plus the per-outlet rows for the 09-17 22:xx UTC batch; a per-outlet × batch pivot of `error_msg` prefixes across all four batches to spot flapping; `crawler_health/2026-09-18.json` vs. the rows; `summary_line()` re-derived from the JSON vs. the message the operator pasted; `Get-WinEvent -ListLog Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational` for a scheduled-vs-manual trigger record.
- **Result:**
  - Task Scheduler: `LastRunTime 18/09/2026 05:30:02`, `LastTaskResult 1`, `NextRunTime 19/09/2026 05:30`, `NumberOfMissedRuns 0`; settings unchanged from the 09-17 fix (`DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=False StopIfGoingOnBatteries=False StartWhenAvailable=True WakeToRun=True PT2H`), principal `bdrex/Interactive/Limited`.
  - `crawl_log`: exactly one batch that morning — `log_id 277–308`, `2026-09-17 22:32:10.712900 UTC` (05:32:10 WIB), 32 rows, 21 completed / 11 failed. The other three batches are the documented manual triggers (09-16 22:47, 23:42; 09-17 00:21 UTC). No manual run on 09-18.
  - JSON `generated_at 2026-09-18T05:32:10`, 32 results, row-for-row equal to `crawl_log`. Message text byte-identical to `summary_line()` output: `OK=15, FALLBACK=6, PARTIAL=0, BROKEN=10, ERROR=1 (of 32) | needs attention: <11 unhealthy>, <6 FALLBACK>`.
  - Gap check: 22.1 h since the prior row → below the 36 h threshold → correctly silent (only the ⚠️ arrived, no ⏰).
  - Pivot: per-outlet status **identical** to the 09-17 07:21 WIB run. The only flips in history are batch 1→2 (CNBC/CNN/KONTAN/KUMPARAN/PIKIRANRAKYAT `OK→FALLBACK`, when OPS-02's classification landed; OKEZONE one-off `ERROR` in batch 2). All 17 flagged outlets map to open tickets: SRC-02 ×5, SRC-03 ×2, SRC-04, SRC-05, SRC-06 ×2, SRC-07 ×2, SRC-08 ×4 = 17. Nothing new broke.
  - `Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational` is **disabled** (`IsEnabled=False`) — no event 107/110 record exists to distinguish a scheduled trigger from a manual start after the fact. Today's proof is `LastRunTime`=05:30:02 + the single batch. `wevtutil sl Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational /e:true` (admin) would make future audits definitive; noted on the ticket, not filed.
  - Two things the evidence itself exposed: (a) all 128 `health_check` rows ever written have `started_at = finished_at` to the microsecond — `log_to_db` sets only `finished_at = NOW()` and `started_at` falls back to `DEFAULT NOW()`, both the logging transaction's start, i.e. the *end* of the run (today: rows say 05:32:10, the task started 05:30:02) → **OPS-12 (#75)**, P3. (b) The alert lists the same 17 already-ticketed outlets it will list every morning until SRC-02..08 land, with no "new vs. known" distinction — the shape of alert that stops being read → **OPS-13 (#76)**, enhancement: diff against the previous per-outlet `crawl_log` row.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** OPS-01's Done-when is *observed*, and closed on that basis. Unexercised residuals: catch-up after a genuinely missed window (`StartWhenAvailable` on a `CalendarTrigger` with no `EndBoundary`) — if it doesn't queue, the next run's 36 h gap check fires at ~48 h, so a missed day is late, not silent; `LogonType=Interactive` still requires a logged-on session. The general lesson: a monitoring ticket's Done-when is the *observation*, and the first observation is also the moment to read the alert as its recipient will — "correct and delivered" is not the same as "actionable tomorrow".
- **Links:** OPS-01 (#9) **closed** · OPS-12 (#75), OPS-13 (#76) filed · `crawler_health/2026-09-18.json` (gitignored) · `crawl_log` log_id 277–308

### 2026-09-18 — TEMPO: where do the 60 s go?
- **Question / hypothesis:** every `health_check` batch since 09-16 reports TEMPO as `ERROR: timeout after 60s`, but SRC-06 (#17) was filed on `BROKEN: branch "www" parsed to 0 links` (09-15). Is tempo.co down/slow, or is the crawler burning the budget before `_scrape`? The traps say not to retry with a bigger timeout, so time each stage once instead.
- **Method:** inline probe with the crawler's own `page_loader`: time `get_soup(URL.TEMPO)`; count `<sitemap><loc>` entries matching `-sitemap.xml`; time the first three branch fetches individually and count their `<url>`; then time `TempoCrawler._get_branches(index_soup)` end to end and print what it returns.
- **Result:** index `https://www.tempo.co/sitemap.xml` 0.4 s, 28 entries, all `-sitemap.xml` (`pages`, `politik`, `hukum`, `ekonomi`, `lingkungan`, …). `pages-sitemap.xml` 0.5 s / 47 `<url>`, `politik-sitemap.xml` 0.5 s / 200, `hukum-sitemap.xml` 0.4 s / 200. **`_get_branches`: 155.2 s → 3 branches**, and the three are article URLs (`/newsletter/kebenaran-yang-semakin-mahal-…-2287657`, `/prelude/sampul-…`, `/info-tempo/…`) under the keys `www`, `www.tempo.co/prelude/sampul`, `www.tempo.co/info`. Cause, `tempo_crawler.py:23-43`: `_get_branches` does a `get_soup` per entry (28 sequential fetches — 155 s in the loop although each is ~0.5 s alone; throttling or a slow branch, not isolated, moot once the loop is removed) and treats each leaf sitemap as a sub-index, taking its *first `<loc>`* (an article) as the branch link and deriving the name with `re.sub(r"(.*)(//)(.*)(.tempo)(.*)", r"\3", …)`, which collapses 28 entries into 3 keys. `check_crawlers.py`'s 60 s per-outlet budget expires inside `_get_branches` → `ERROR`; a real `task=sitemap` run has no cap, so it would take 155 s and then `_scrape` an article page → 0 `<url>` → the "0 links" SRC-06 describes.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** same root cause as SRC-06, one layer earlier; the health-check `ERROR` is a timing artifact of a `_get_branches` that shouldn't be fetching at all. Fix direction: return `{section: <section>-sitemap.xml}` straight from the index (filter to `ekonomi`/finance sections) and let the standard `_scrape` read the 200-entry leaf. Don't chase the 155 s. Verify with `--report-dir <scratch>` (OPS-10).
- **Links:** SRC-06 (#17), commented · `newscrawler/infrastructure/datasource/scrapers/tempo/tempo_crawler.py`

### 2026-09-18 — OPS-12 + OPS-13: per-outlet timestamps and a delta-first health alert
- **Question / hypothesis:** (1) OPS-12: can `check_crawlers.py --log-db` record each outlet's real check window in `crawl_log` — and, since `started_at`/`finished_at` are `timestamp without time zone` columns the pipeline fills with `NOW()`, how does a Python timestamp have to be bound so it lands in the same frame (the 36 h gap check does `NOW() - MAX(finished_at)` in SQL and would silently break on a 7 h offset)? (2) OPS-13: with the per-outlet baseline one `DISTINCT ON (website)` away, does leading the alert with what *moved* produce a message the operator can act on, and does the whole wrapper path (real subprocess → `--json` → decision → Telegram text) survive the emoji on Windows?
- **Method:** timezone probe on `newsaggregator_test`, rolled back: `INSERT … VALUES (:aware_utc_datetime, NOW()) RETURNING started_at, finished_at, started_at AT TIME ZONE current_setting('TimeZone')`. Then, after the code change: `pytest tests/unit` + `tests/integration` (new `tests/integration/test_check_crawlers_crawl_log_live.py`); `check_crawlers.py --website KOMPAS,DETIK,BISNIS,CNBC --diff-db --report-dir <scratch>` (baseline untouched); `--website KOMPAS,BISNIS --diff-db --json` piped into the wrapper's `_parse_report`/`decide_notification`; a full `--quiet --log-db --report-dir <scratch>` run timed against `date -u`; `run_scheduled_health_check.main()` for real with only `notify_telegram.send` stubbed to capture the message; the two new OPERATIONS.md queries against production.
- **Result:**
  - Server `TimeZone = UTC`, psycopg2 2.9.6. An aware `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` bound to the `timestamp` column stored `23:03:07.713094`, `NOW()` in the same statement `23:03:07.711637` — identical frame (1.5 ms apart from call order). `AT TIME ZONE current_setting('TimeZone')` → `.astimezone()` gives `06:03 +07:00`, the right local instant. A naive `datetime.now()` would have stored `06:03` verbatim into a UTC column.
  - Full `--log-db` run: process start `23:14:16 UTC`, `MIN(started_at) 23:14:17.85` (module import), `MAX(finished_at) 23:15:25.85`; 32 rows, **0 with `started_at = finished_at`** (all 128 older rows still have it); durations 0.0–21.2 s for 31 outlets, avg 3.7 s; TEMPO `ERROR: timeout after 60s` = **65.9 s** (60 s budget + the sequential `fut.result()` wait reaching its future). The 70 s wall time is 60 s of waiting for TEMPO (SRC-06). SUARA's 0.0 s is a 1 ms `get_soup → None`, i.e. real.
  - Delta on the 4-outlet run: `Crawler health 2026-09-18: no change since 2026-09-18 05:32 — 2 known (1 BROKEN, 1 FALLBACK)`, correctly against the 05:32 WIB scheduled batch. On the full run: `🔴 NEW: IDXCHANNEL (BROKEN, was OK) · unchanged: 17 known (10 BROKEN, 1 ERROR, 6 FALLBACK)` — IDXCHANNEL had been OK at 05:32; `first branch scraped 0 article links` after 21.2 s. Solo re-check 3 min later (`--diff-db`, no `--log-db`): `OK`, 100 links, 1.6 s → `🟢 RECOVERED: IDXCHANNEL (OK, was BROKEN)`. A transient under 8-way concurrency, not SRC material — but exactly the shape the delta will surface; the wrapper dry run 2 min after that read `🟢 RECOVERED: IDXCHANNEL … · unchanged: 17 known` and would have sent it under the ⚠️ headline (exit 1 from the 11 unhealthy). The OPERATIONS.md last-two-rows query returns the same single flip.
  - Encoding: my own scratch printer crashed with `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec` on the 🔴 headline — the same thing the wrapper's `logger.info(summary)` would do under Task Scheduler's cp1252 stderr. Fixed by `reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")` on the wrapper's streams and `encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"` on the `subprocess.run` (the child's stderr carries crawler log lines in whatever encoding it had).
  - Suites: `tests/unit` 347 → 388 passed (+41), `tests/integration` 75 passed / 9 xfailed (SYS-10's module, unchanged) including the 3 new live tests. Unit coverage per Done-when: `log_to_db` binds `:started`/`:finished` and the SQL has no `NOW()`; every `check_one` exit path stamps both; the timeout row carries its own window; `classify_change` table incl. OK→FALLBACK = NEW; `compute_delta` new/recovered/changed/unchanged/baseline, per-outlet baseline, pure; `summary_line` first-line assertions for change / no-change / first-run / all-green and byte-equal legacy format; wrapper `decide_notification` for all 4 exit×delta cells plus the knob and the crash-trace fallback.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** bind `crawl_log` timestamps as aware datetimes (rule added to the traps). Compare per outlet, never per batch — the wrapper's own `--log-db` run and any manual `--website X --log-db` would otherwise turn 31 outlets into "baseline" the next morning. Ship the delta *without* multi-run flap suppression: a same-day `--website X --diff-db` settles a surprising NEW in seconds, and hiding a first occurrence is how a real breakage gets a day's head start. Reading the alert as its recipient (the OPS-01 lesson) also applied to the *log*: an emoji in a message you never print is free; the moment it's logged on Windows it's a traceback.
- **Links:** OPS-12 (#75) fixed · OPS-13 (#76) fixed · `scripts/check_crawlers.py`, `scripts/run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/unit/test_check_crawlers.py`, `tests/unit/test_run_scheduled_health_check.py`, `tests/integration/test_check_crawlers_crawl_log_live.py` · `crawl_log` log_id 309–340 (first real-window batch), 341–372 (wrapper dry run) · OPERATIONS.md "Which outlet is slow, and what changed"

### 2026-09-18 — P2-medium sweep: 9 tickets fixed, 2 more filed and fixed along the way, OPS-04 built and DBT-20 partially resolved
- **Question / hypothesis:** work through the P2-medium backlog (19 open) rather than the per-outlet SRC-02..08 scraper tickets, which need live network probing per site and don't fit in the same pass. Two (SYS-14 #33, DBT-07 #34) were already marked "Fixed" in this file from a prior session but still showed as open GitHub issues — real, or just unclosed?
- **Method:** `git log --oneline --all | grep -iE "SYS-14|DBT-07"` found both commits, each with its own `Fixes #N` footer, already on `dev` — GitHub only auto-closes via commit footer on the *default* branch, and `dev` has never been merged to `main`, so the closes never fired. Re-verified both live (`tests/unit/test_soft404_detection.py` 6 passed; production `count(*)-count(distinct sitemap_id) from articles` = 0, `articles_sitemap_id_uq` present) and closed them directly — not "fixed today," just unblocked from the ticket tracker. For each of the remaining 9: read the ticket in full, implement, write/extend unit tests, mutation-test the fix (revert the code, confirm the new test goes red, restore), run the full `tests/unit`+`tests/integration` suites, and where the ticket named a live DB check, run it for real against `newsaggregator`/`newsaggregator_test` and quote the output in the closing comment.
- **Result (chronological, each already has its own commit + closing comment with full evidence):**
  - **SYS-26 (#71) fixed** (`31ce455`): DBT-09's `seen_this_batch` dedup guard only covered the new-chunk branch of `_embed_batch`; a key already in FAISS (the SYS-17 recovery path — exactly DBT-20's orphan shape) skipped the guard entirely, so two chunks sharing that key both reached `result`, and `save_chunks`' own unique index then aborted the whole batch. Reproduced the exact `UniqueViolation` on the pre-fix code against `newsaggregator_test`, fixed by moving the guard before the branch split.
  - **SYS-10 (#28), SYS-27 (#77), SYS-28 (#78) fixed** (`bb93a9e`): `backfill_chunks.py` (target of SYS-10) had *three* independent bugs stacked on each other, each hidden behind the last — fixing the `nomic-embed-text` 401 (SYS-10) exposed `HierarchicalChunker.chunk()`'s interface having moved on to DTOs while the script still called the old `(list, dict)` shape (SYS-27, filed mid-fix), and fixing *that* exposed `--dry-run` looping forever because it never writes anything to shrink its own `WHERE chunk_id IS NULL` fetch query (SYS-28, filed mid-fix — caught by a genuinely hung `pytest` process, 20+ minutes, killed via `TaskStop` and reproduced standalone outside pytest to confirm it wasn't a fluke). All three needed fixing before `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py` (`xfail` since DBT-06) could actually pass. **Lesson: an `xfail(strict=False)` module can hide more than one bug behind the first exception** — don't assume the ticket that unblocks it is the only fix needed; keep going until the suite is green, filing what you find as you go.
  - **SYS-11 (#30) fixed** (`2379549`): `Crawler._get_timestamp` re-expressed `DateTimeReader.convert_date()`'s already-correct `+07:00` (WIB) instant in UTC before returning it, shifting any 00:00–06:59 WIB article onto the previous stored calendar day. `emitennews_crawler.py`'s own override already had a comment naming this exact bug and working around it — the fix was to make the base class match. `test_date_time_reader.py::DT07` had been asserting the *buggy* conversion as if it were expected crawler behavior; renamed and re-scoped rather than left contradicting the new regression guard.
  - **SYS-21 (#42) fixed** (`52f68b4`): `crawl_website`'s if/elif chain had no `else`, and `crawl_websites_in_batch` treated "returned without exception" as success — a typo'd `CRAWL_TASK` or `task=None` ran the whole website list, called zero service methods, wrote no `crawl_log` row, exited 0. Added `VALID_TASKS`/`CRAWLER_DICT` validation up front in `crawl_websites_in_batch` (before any site is touched) plus an `else: raise` backstop in `crawl_website` itself.
  - **SYS-09 (#27) fixed** (`3aaa506`): a sitemap whose fetch/extraction failed was never stamped `last_crawl_attempt` (only `mark_sitemaps_attempted`'s successes are), so it re-occupied a `load_all_sitemaps` slot every run forever. Added `sitemaps.fetch_failures`/`last_fetch_error` (migration, applied to both DBs), computed the failed set as `batch` minus what `batch_crawling_details` actually returned (no change needed to `_get_news_details`'s return type), and excluded `fetch_failures >= SITEMAP_MAX_FETCH_FAILURES` (default 3) from the query. `increment_fetch_failures` also stamps the cooldown, not just the counter — otherwise "one fewer failure" would still look ineligible in a same-second test (caught this while writing the live test, fixed the test's backdating rather than the code).
  - **OPS-04 (#52) partially built** (`c1e14ff`), **left open**: `scripts/check_store_consistency.py` (detection only — table/JSON/exit-code, same contract as `check_crawlers.py`, reads both FAISS docstores via pickle so it needs no embedding model) plus `/api/health`'s new `faiss_docs` field, read the same lightweight way specifically so it can't become another DBT-15 blocking hazard. Live: 4/9 checks nonzero, all at-or-below the 2026-09-16 audit baseline, none growing. The ticket's `--repair` mode (delete/rewrite FAISS + Postgres data) deliberately not built this pass — that's a dry-run+backup+decision session, not a first-pass addition to a detection tool.
  - **DBT-20 (#70) fixed** (`f732861`), using OPS-04's own tooling to find the population: 582 of MEDIAINDONESIA's 1,183 orphaned-L1 sitemaps had been marked `rejected_filter` by `backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`'s headline-only re-check, contradicting direct evidence (a real L1 vector only exists if the original filter passed). `scripts/fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py` flips any `rejected_filter` row with FAISS evidence back to `pending` — general, not MEDIAINDONESIA-specific. Decision on the harder question (re-crawl vs. accept the lower-fidelity FAISS text vs. write off): re-crawl, matching the VIVA/SYS-08 precedent, executed via *nothing but the status correction* — a `pending` sitemap already re-enters the same queue every other pending sitemap uses, so the next scheduled `task=all` run does the actual re-crawl with no dedicated script needed.
  - **OPS-03 (#11) fixed** (`13decfe`): `check_one` always sampled `next(iter(branches.items()))` — whichever branch a sitemap index lists first, often not finance (GRIDID's "Bobo", PIKIRANRAKYAT's "AksaraJabar", DETIK's "suara-pembaca"). `pick_branch()` prefers a name matching `ekonomi|bisnis|market|finance|keuangan|money|uang`. Live-verified against the exact two outlets the ticket named: PIKIRANRAKYAT now samples "PotensiBisnis", DETIK now samples "berita-ekonomi".
  - Suites, cumulative across the whole sweep: `tests/unit` 347 → 436 passed (+89), `tests/integration` 75 passed/9 xfailed → 88 passed/0 xfailed.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** a commit with `Fixes #N` in its footer does not close the issue unless the branch it's on is (or gets merged to) the repo's default branch — check `git log` before assuming a ticket needs work; this repo's convention keeps `dev` as the long-lived work branch, so this will keep happening until/unless that changes. Fixing the *named* bug in a ticket is not the same as making its Done-when true when the Done-when is "the test suite passes" — a stale/broken script can have several independent bugs stacked where only the outermost one is visible until the one under it is fixed too (SYS-10/27/28). A repair tool's *detection* half and its *repair* half are different risk classes and don't need to ship together — OPS-04's read-only script shipped today; its data-mutating `--repair` mode did not, on purpose.
- **Links:** SYS-14 (#33), DBT-07 (#34) closed (already fixed, tracker gap) · SYS-26 (#71), SYS-10 (#28), SYS-27 (#77), SYS-28 (#78), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-21 (#42), SYS-09 (#27), DBT-20 (#70), OPS-03 (#11) fixed and closed · OPS-04 (#52) open, partial · commits `31ce455`, `bb93a9e`, `2379549`, `52f68b4`, `3aaa506`, `c1e14ff`, `f732861`, `13decfe`
### 2026-09-18 — QA audit of the P2-medium sweep (SYS-26, SYS-10/27/28, SYS-11, SYS-21, SYS-09, OPS-04, DBT-20, OPS-03, OPS-12/13): 1 P1 filed, 1 reopened, 5 more tickets
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev handed over 11 closed tickets plus OPS-04's detection half, all committed on `dev` between `b5ba6fc` and `e060d3f`, with "tests/unit 436, tests/integration 88 passed, 0 xfailed". Does each fix hold under mutation, against the production DB, and against the way the pipeline is actually run — not just against the ticket's own test?
- **Method:** both suites as-is; every commit diff read; a byte-level mutation harness (scratch `mutate.py`) re-introducing 19 faithful mutants and running the named guard suite for each, restoring via `git checkout`; production `newsaggregator` read-only via psycopg2 (`crawl_log` cadence, `sitemaps` schema/indexes/`crawl_status`/`fetch_failures`, `chunks.article_id`, duplicate links); `Get-ScheduledTask` for what actually runs unattended; the team's own tools run for real (`check_store_consistency.py`, `fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py --dry-run`, `backfill_chunks.py --dry-run`); a `backfill_chunks.py` real-mode repro on `newsaggregator_test` with a scratch `VECTOR_DB_DIR`; a network probe (`scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py`) fetching all 21 HEALTHY sources' live sitemaps through the real crawlers and comparing today's `(link, posted_at)` to the stored rows; `tracemalloc` timing of `web.app._faiss_doc_counts()`.
- **Result:**
  - **Suites:** unit 436 passed, integration 88 passed — matches the claim. `git status` clean after every mutation.
  - **Mutation: 17/19 caught.** SYS-26 (guard back on the `elif`), SYS-09 ×3 (drop the SQL clause — unit-green but the live integration test catches it, as the dev said; never call `increment_fetch_failures`; drop the cooldown stamp — same, integration-only), SYS-11 (`.astimezone(UTC)` back), SYS-21 ×3, OPS-03, OPS-12, OPS-13 ×2, SYS-10 (`nomic-embed-text` back). **Survivors:** SYS-28 — removing the keyset cursor makes `test_backfill_pipeline.py` *hang* (killed at 240 s; no `pytest-timeout` in the venv), i.e. the guard reproduces the bug instead of failing; DBT-20 — removing `AND sitemap_id = ANY(:ids)` from `find_misclassified` (the script would flip all 24,413 `rejected_filter` rows) leaves 436 green. → **DBT-25 (#82)**.
  - **Nothing in this batch has run for real.** `crawl_log` has no `sitemap`/`full_text` row since 2026-09-17 12:32 UTC; the last full-list `task=all` is 2026-06-02; the only registered scheduled job is the 05:30 health check. `fetch_failures` = 0 on all 101,492 rows.
  - **SYS-11 (#30) — code correct, consequence missed → SYS-29 (#84), P1.** `save_sitemaps` dedups on `(link, int(posted_at))` in a Python map with no unique index. The fix moves `posted_at` forward one day for every 00:00–06:59 WIB article, so every such article already stored and still listed dedup-misses and inserts a second row. Live probe across all 21 HEALTHY sources: **4,024 would-insert duplicates on the next crawl, 3,817 of them exactly the +1-day shift** (VIVA 2,511, JPNN 816, TVONENEWS 161, PIKIRANRAKYAT 150, IDXCHANNEL 83, TRIBUN 35, WARTAEKONOMI 26, CNBC 19, GRIDID 12, SINDONEWS 4); 207 (OKEZONE 206, GRIDID 1) are sites re-stamping `news:publication_date`, a pre-existing hole in the same key. The chain is already visible once in production: sindonews `sitemaps` 8478/85673 (same URL, 20260513/20260602) → `articles` 3182/11990, identical 2,688 chars, 5 chunk rows each. Fix direction: dedup on `link`, `UNIQUE(sitemaps.link)` after deduping the 2 existing links.
  - **SYS-10/27/28 (#28/#77/#78) — Done-when met, tool still unusable → DBT-24 (#79), P2.** `_FETCH_SQL` joins on `chunks.article_id`, which the live pipeline never sets (0 of 101,157 non-NULL), so `--dry-run` on production selects **`11961 articles → 27432 chunks`** while articles genuinely without chunks (by `sitemap_id`) = 0. Real mode on `newsaggregator_test` against one live-style chunked article: loads the model, embeds, then `UniqueViolation … chunks_sitemap_level_index_uq` — nothing written only because DBT-09's index exists. Vectors would go to `VECTOR_DB_DIR/faiss_l1.index`, which nothing reads (the commit's own docstring says so and defers to DBT-10, which doesn't track it). `_parse_paragraphs` treats post-SYS-18 prose as one paragraph → ~1.3 L2 chunks/article vs. the live ~8.5; the integration test seeds `json.dumps(list)`, never the production format.
  - **OPS-04 (#52) — detection tool works, `/api/health` regressed → OPS-14 (#80), P2.** `check_store_consistency.py` reproduced the claimed 4/9 nonzero, all KNOWN, none GROWING. But `_faiss_doc_counts()` `pickle.load`s both docstores (37 MB + 83 MB) on every request, inside an `async def`: **2.06 / 2.13 / 2.09 s, 304 MB peak** ×3 — a per-request event-loop stall that grows with the corpus, introduced while citing DBT-15 as the thing being avoided.
  - **DBT-20 (#70) — reopened.** Status flip verified (`--dry-run` → "Nothing to fix"; MEDIAINDONESIA `rejected_filter` 825→243, `pending` 11,561→12,143). Done-when says "decision made **and executed**"; the closing comment executes it via "the next scheduled task=all run (06:00/18:00 daily)", which is not registered anywhere, and the 582 rows (all ≤ 2026-06-02) sit in a 12,143-row `LIMIT 1000` `posted_at DESC` queue for the one serially-crawled source. Enabled, not executed.
  - **SYS-09 (#27) — correct, incomplete → OPS-15 (#81), P3.** The cap is permanent with no reset script (OPS-09 built one for the identical Gemini shape one day earlier), the failed set is "batch minus DTOs" so a source-wide block or a `_get_news_details` regression retires 1,000 rows/run/source after 3 runs ≥ 7 days apart, and `last_fetch_error` is always the same literal. Process: CLAUDE.md's migration list ("must match `scripts/migrate.py`") and OPERATIONS.md's env table were not updated; the comment at `crawler_service_impl.py:116-118` now contradicts the code below it.
  - **SYS-21 (#42) — verified; one residual → SYS-30 (#83), P3.** `crawl_websites_in_batch([], 'all')` (what `process_event({'task': 'all'})` produces) is still `ok=0 failed=0`, zero service calls, exit 0.
  - **SYS-26 (#71), OPS-03 (#11), OPS-12/13 (#75/#76): verified, stay closed.** SYS-26's guard placement is right and the live orphan-repair test exercises it. OPS-03 only changes the *sampled* branch; expect one morning of NEW/RECOVERED noise as PIKIRANRAKYAT/DETIK diff against their old-branch baseline. OPS-12/13's wrapper handles the no-JSON crash path (`report=None` → raw text sent).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **"Verified live" without a `task=all` since the fix means verified in a REPL** — every fix in this batch was tested against the test DB or a hand-run; the pipeline that exercises them last ran on 2026-06-02 and is not scheduled. (2) **When a fix changes how a stored value is derived, grep for every dedup/lookup key that contains that value before closing** — SYS-11's one-line fix is a 3,817-row duplication event waiting for the next crawl. (3) **A script's Done-when of "its test module passes" proves nothing about production when the test seeds its own data format** — run `--dry-run` against `newsaggregator` and check the count is plausible. (4) **Mutation-testing an infinite-loop fix needs `pytest-timeout`** or the guard is the bug. (5) **A closing comment that cites a schedule must name the registered job**, not the runbook's recommendation.
- **Links:** filed SYS-29 (#84, P1), DBT-24 (#79), OPS-14 (#80), OPS-15 (#81), DBT-25 (#82), SYS-30 (#83) · reopened DBT-20 (#70) · comments on SYS-10 (#28), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-21 (#42) · verified closed: SYS-26 (#71), SYS-21 (#42), SYS-11 (#30), SYS-09 (#27), OPS-03 (#11), OPS-12 (#75), OPS-13 (#76), SYS-10/27/28 (#28/#77/#78) · `scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py` (new, reusable — SYS-29's Done-when check; exit 1 while any source would duplicate); the mutation harness, `backfill_chunks.py` repro and `/api/health` timing were throwaway scratch, reproduced inline above
### 2026-09-18 — SYS-29: make the sitemap dedup key the link alone and enforce it
- **Question / hypothesis:** the QA batch of 2026-09-18 (SYS-29 P1, OPS-14, DBT-24, DBT-20 reopened, SYS-30, DBT-25, OPS-15) — fix each properly: read the ticket, fix, write the guard, mutate the fix and watch the guard go red, run both suites, run the ticket's own live check against `newsaggregator`, and only then close. SYS-29 first because it blocks every `task=sitemap`/`task=all`. (Heading kept on the first ticket so the trap-line anchors resolve; this entry covers the whole batch. Work ran 2026-09-18 evening into 2026-09-19 morning.)
- **Method:** per ticket, in order —
  - **SYS-29 (#84):** `save_sitemaps` keyed on `(link, int(posted_at))` and pre-fetched by a `posted_at >= earliest-in-batch` window. Changed both: key is `link`, lookup is `link IN (...)` chunked by 1,000 (`find_existing_by_link`, public so the probe uses the real code), `posted_at` on an existing row is left as first seen (decision, documented in the code: `articles.meta_data.posted_at` was derived the same way, and re-stamping sites would churn it). New `scripts/dedupe_sitemap_links.py` (keeper rule = entity_mentions-referenced → has article/knowledge_extracted → has chunks → lowest id; full-row JSON backup; SQL in one transaction *then* FAISS, `--faiss-only` resume — DBT-21's critique applied) and `scripts/migrate_sitemaps_unique_link.sql` (`sitemaps_link_uq`), registered in `migrate.py`/CLAUDE.md; ORM `Index("sitemaps_link_uq", ..., unique=True)`. `scripts/experiments/probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py` rewritten to ask `find_existing_by_link` instead of re-deriving the key.
  - **OPS-14 (#80):** `/api/health`'s count now reads `faiss.read_index(index.faiss, IO_FLAG_MMAP | IO_FLAG_READ_ONLY).ntotal`, cached on the file's `(mtime_ns, size)`; the payload runs via `run_in_threadpool`. Timed the alternatives first (scratch `time_faiss_count.py`).
  - **DBT-24 (#79):** deleted `scripts/backfill_chunks.py`, `tests/integration/test_backfill_pipeline.py`, `tests/unit/test_embedding_id.py`, `tests/unit/test_parse_paragraphs.py`; `backfill_orphan_chunks.py` gets `main(argv) -> summary` and `tests/integration/test_backfill_orphan_chunks_live.py` (production prose format, no-op on a chunked article, level-aware selection, `search_l2()` finds the vectors); PRD §7.5 marked superseded.
  - **SYS-30 (#83):** `crawl_websites_in_batch` raises `ValueError` for an empty website list on a website task; tests down to `process_event({"task": "all"})`.
  - **DBT-25 (#82):** `pytest-timeout==2.4.0` installed + pinned, `pytest.ini` `timeout = 120` / `timeout_method = thread`; verified a 200 s sleep test dies in ~5 s with exit 1. `tests/integration/test_fix_rejected_filter_live.py` seeds two `rejected_filter` rows with one in the L1 set. SYS-28's mutant is moot (script deleted under DBT-24).
  - **OPS-15 (#81):** `RequestsPageLoader.get_url_with_reason`/`get_soup_with_reason` (old names are wrappers), `Crawler._fetch_news_details` returns `(dto, reason)`, `batch_crawling_details` returns `NewsDetailsBatch` (a `list` with `.failures`, the `SitemapSaveResult` pattern), `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` buffers failures for the run and `_record_fetch_failures` either logs ERROR and counts nothing (≥ `SITEMAP_SOURCE_FAILURE_RATIO`, default 1.0) or calls `increment_fetch_failures` once per distinct reason. `scripts/reset_fetch_failures.py` (`--website/--all-retired/--like/--sitemap-ids`, `--clear-cooldown`) + live test. Docs: CLAUDE.md migration list (SYS-09's file was missing), OPERATIONS.md env table (`SITEMAP_RECRAWL_COOLDOWN_DAYS`, `SITEMAP_MAX_FETCH_FAILURES`, `SITEMAP_SOURCE_FAILURE_RATIO`), the stale `crawler_service_impl.py` comment.
  - **DBT-20 (#70):** `scripts/recrawl_faiss_orphans.py --website MEDIAINDONESIA` selects the L1-vector-but-nothing-else sitemaps and hands them to `crawl_newsdetails(specific_sitemaps=...)`, bypassing the 12,143-row queue. Filed **DBT-26 (#85)** for item 3.
  - Mutation harness (scratch `mutate.py`, string-substitution + restore-from-copy since the files were uncommitted): 5 SYS-29 mutants, 4 OPS-14, 1 SYS-30, 1 DBT-25 (the QA survivor), 8 OPS-15.
- **Result:**
  - **SYS-29:** production had exactly the 2 duplicate links QA found. Applied for real (`backups/sys29_dedupe_sitemap_links_20260918T135953Z.json`): dropped sitemap 85673 (SINDONEWS twin of 8478: 1 article, 5 chunks, 1 L1 + 4 L2 vectors) and 86871 (VIVA, bare); `sitemaps` 101,492 → 101,490 = distinct links; `sitemaps_link_uq` on both DBs; `check_store_consistency.py` unchanged apart from L1 22,591 → 22,590 and L2 79,749 → 79,745. Probe over all 21 HEALTHY sources: **`would_insert_duplicate=0` everywhere, `recognised == already_in_db` everywhere, exit 0**; `date_drift=3,994` (3,787 SYS-11 shifts — VIVA 2,508, JPNN 816, TVONENEWS 161, PIKIRANRAKYAT 150, IDXCHANNEL 68 …; OKEZONE 206 re-stamps) matches QA's 4,024/3,817 to within the day's listing churn. Integration suite exposed that two fixtures (`test_schema._insert_sitemap`, `test_data_sources.make_sitemap_model`) defaulted every row to `http://example.com` — they were leaning on the missing constraint; now a fresh link per call. 5/5 mutants caught.
  - **OPS-14:** `_faiss_doc_counts` 1,059 + 1,561 ms / 98 + 224 MB (pickle) → 13 + 44 ms cold, **0.18 ms warm**, no allocation; counts identical (22,590 / 79,745). Endpoint p50 **96.8 ms** over 7 calls with the production index (the remaining ~95 ms is `get_stats()`'s Postgres COUNTs, pre-existing). 4/4 mutants caught, including "pickle.load is back".
  - **DBT-24:** `backfill_orphan_chunks.py --dry-run` on production → `0 orphan article(s)`, matching `check_store_consistency.py`'s `articles_no_chunks = 0`. The new live test: a 3-paragraph prose article → 1 L1 + ≥2 L2 chunks, all `embedding_id`s present in the docstores, `search_l2("<marker> paragraf 1")` returns the sitemap; second run `{"selected": 0, ...}`; an L1-only article gets only L2 and keeps its seeded L1 row.
  - **SYS-30:** 1/1 mutant caught; `process_event({"task": "all"}, None)` raises `ValueError("task 'all' needs at least one website ...")`.
  - **DBT-25:** the exact QA survivor (`AND sitemap_id = ANY(:ids)` removed) is now red in the live test (and in the unit test, which asserts the clause text). Timeout verified end-to-end.
  - **OPS-15:** 8/8 mutants caught. Live probe through the real CNBC crawler: a made-up path → `'HTTP 404'`, `no-such-host.invalid` → `'ConnectionError after 1 retries'`. The SYS-09 unit test that asserted "every batch failing is still counted" was deliberately re-scoped: with 3 sitemaps → 3 batches of 1, all-fail is now a source-wide failure by definition; the SYS-09 case (one batch dead, others fine) is kept and still counted.
  - **DBT-20:** re-crawl started 2026-09-19 07:18 WIB as a detached process (the Bash tool's 10-minute cap would have killed a background job mid-`save_local` — DBT-10's exact corruption window; stopped the first attempt during model load, nothing written, relaunched with `Start-Process`). Result: **attempted=1183 extracted=1183 passed_filter=405 saved=405** in 18.7 min (`crawl_log` 405, `full_text`, completed), 0 fetch failures; MEDIAINDONESIA `articles` 96 → 501; L2 vectors 79,745 → 81,654; L1 unchanged at 22,590 with "2+ L1 vectors" still 0 — the SYS-17/SYS-26 reuse path did its job. The other 778 were fetched fine but rejected by today's title+body filter (CSR awards, tree planting, digital-payment op-eds under `/ekonomi/` — legitimately below 0.35), leaving them `rejected_filter` *with* an orphan L1 vector: exactly the contradiction the ticket's Done-when forbids, and one `fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py` would flip back forever. With the user's go-ahead, `--write-off-remaining` deleted those 778 L1 vectors (docstore entries backed up to `backups/dbt20_written_off_l1_20260919T003834Z.json`). After: `check_store_consistency.py` `l1_no_chunks` 1,183 → 778 → **0**, `l1_not_in_l2` → **0**, 4/9 → 2/9 nonzero (only the 9,447 VIVA no-article orphans remain, the deferred full-VIVA backfill); `fix_rejected_filter_with_faiss_evidence.py --dry-run` → "Nothing to fix"; both stores `ntotal == docstore == mapping` and vector↔text cosine 1.0000 on 150 random samples each (L1 21,812, L2 81,654). **Why only 34 % recovered:** these 1,183 passed the filter in June 2026 and were rejected today with the same INTERESTS/threshold — the June extraction very likely fed different text to the filter (the site extractor or a fallback returning boilerplate); not investigated further, the vectors are gone and the pages are off-topic by today's rule.
  - Suites: `tests/unit` 436 → **460 passed** (−17 deleted with DBT-24, +41 new), `tests/integration` 88 → **102 passed** (−9 with DBT-24, +23 new), both run twice.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **A `list` subclass that carries extra data is this codebase's way to add information to a return value without touching callers** — `SitemapSaveResult` (SYS-07), now `NewsDetailsBatch` (OPS-15); mocks returning plain lists keep working via `getattr(..., "failures", None)`. (2) **A test that patches a public method as its seam breaks silently when the implementation starts calling a new primitive** — `test_soft404_detection.py` patched `get_url`; two tests then made real requests to `site.test` and passed only because those expected `None`. When adding a `*_with_reason` primitive, grep the tests for patches of the wrapper. (3) **When a ticket offers "delete or rewrite", check whether the rewrite already exists under another name** — DBT-24's (a)/(b)/(c) described `backfill_orphan_chunks.py`. (4) **A background job that writes FAISS must not run under a tool timeout** — the harness kills it, and `save_local` is not atomic (DBT-10). (5) **The probe that checks a fix must call the fixed code, not a copy of the old logic** — the first `probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py` would have reported 4,024 duplicates forever.
- **Links:** SYS-29 (#84), OPS-14 (#80), DBT-24 (#79), SYS-30 (#83), DBT-25 (#82), OPS-15 (#81) fixed · DBT-20 (#70) re-crawl executed (result above) · DBT-26 (#85) filed · new: `scripts/dedupe_sitemap_links.py`, `scripts/migrate_sitemaps_unique_link.sql`, `scripts/reset_fetch_failures.py`, `scripts/recrawl_faiss_orphans.py`, `pytest.ini` · deleted: `scripts/backfill_chunks.py` (+3 test modules) · commits: the per-ticket commits following `2f3f6c9` on `dev`
### 2026-09-19 — QA audit of the SYS-29 batch (SYS-29, OPS-14, DBT-24, SYS-30, DBT-25, OPS-15, DBT-20): all seven hold; the filter behind DBT-20's "405 recovered" does not
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev closed the seven tickets from the 2026-09-18 QA audit (commits `363933e`..`714bfb8` on `dev`, "tests/unit 460, tests/integration 102", SYS-29 and DBT-20 applied to production). Does each fix hold under mutation, on the production DB, and — for SYS-29, the P1 — on a *real* `task=sitemap` run, which the closing comment had not done? And what did DBT-20's re-crawl actually recover?
- **Method:** both suites as-is; every commit diff read; a byte-level mutation harness (scratch `mutate.py`, `git checkout --` restore, CRLF-aware, `--timeout=120`) with 15 mutants chosen to be *different* from the dev's own list; production `newsaggregator` read-only via psycopg2 (indexes, `schema_migrations`, duplicate links, `fetch_failures`, `crawl_status`, per-source eligible queue, the 405/778 populations by `sitemaps.category`); `web.app._health_payload()` timed with `tracemalloc`; `pytest-timeout` proven with a 30 s sleep test at `--timeout=3`; `reset_fetch_failures.py --all-retired --dry-run` on production; CLAUDE.md migration list diffed against `migrate.py` `MIGRATIONS`; `backups/dbt20_written_off_l1_*.json` inspected; **one real production write:** `CRAWL_WEBSITES=CNBC CRAWL_TASK=sitemap python __main__.py` after `probe_sitemap_dedup_drift.py CNBC`; the 405 re-crawled MEDIAINDONESIA rows and a random 600 of all saved articles scored through the real `SemanticImportanceFilter` exactly as `_crawl_newsdetails_inner` builds the text (new reusable `scripts/experiments/probe_filter_precision.py`).
- **Result:**
  - **Suites:** unit 460 passed, integration 102 passed — matches. `git status` clean after every mutation.
  - **Mutation: 14/14 meaningful mutants caught** (OPS-15 ×8: boundary `>=`→`>`, `_load_page` isinstance short-circuit, generic-exception / soft-404 / extractor-exception reasons dropped, per-reason grouping collapsed, end-of-run call removed, plain-list fallback dropped; SYS-29 ×3: chunk-loop off-by-one, map filtered by source, in-batch duplicate not cached; SYS-30 ×1: `not` → `is None`; OPS-14 ×2: cache never invalidates, `run_in_threadpool` bypassed). One survivor by design: removing OPS-15's 50 % WARNING branch. Best-guarded batch so far.
  - **SYS-29 (#84) — verified on a real crawl.** Probe: CNBC `listed=1200 already_in_db=163 would_insert_duplicate=0 date_drift=19 genuinely_new=1037`. `task=sitemap`: `Saved 1037 new sitemaps` — `sitemaps` 101,490 → 102,527 (+1,037 exactly), `COUNT(DISTINCT link) = COUNT(*)`, the SYS-11-drift URL (`…/20260507061753-29-732911/…`) is still one row at its first-seen 20260506, `crawl_log` 406 `sitemap`/completed/1037, 0 `Failed to save`, 0 `UniqueViolation`. `sitemaps_link_uq` + `schema_migrations` row on both DBs. **Crawls are safe.**
  - **OPS-14 (#80):** `_health_payload()` 355 ms cold / 90 ms warm, `_faiss_doc_counts()` 0.11 ms warm, 9.3 MB peak (was 2.1 s / 304 MB); counts 21,812 / 81,654 match the docstores. **DBT-25 (#82):** `pytest-timeout==2.4.0` pinned; a 30 s sleep dies in 4.9 s, exit 1. **SYS-30 (#83), DBT-24 (#79):** verified as claimed; `backfill_orphan_chunks.py --dry-run` → 0 on production. **OPS-15 (#81):** (a)–(d) verified; CLAUDE.md list == `MIGRATIONS` (14/14, same order); env table complete; reset script runs (0 rows — the counters have still never fired, `fetch_failures > 0` = 0 after the only post-fix run).
  - **DBT-20 (#70) — numbers exact, population mischaracterised.** 96 + 405 = 501 MEDIAINDONESIA articles ✔, 243 + 778 = 1,021 `rejected_filter` ✔, L1 21,812 / L2 81,654 ✔, `articles_no_chunks` 0 ✔. But the 778 written off were **`fashion` 406 / `ekonomi` 177 / `forum-mahasiswa` 104 / `hiburan` 91**, not "CSR awards … under `/ekonomi/`", and **778/778** L1 texts begin with the same page widget (`Headline / Fundamental ekonomi kita bagus di semua sisi … inflasi terkendali, investasi bagus.` twice) — the "June extraction fed boilerplate" guess is confirmed by the backup itself. And **108 of the 405 "recovered" (27 %) are `fashion` 65 / `forum-mahasiswa` 37 / `hiburan` 6** — "Jessica Mila Kagumi Koleksi Baru Biyan" (0.351 vs `Rupiah`), "Alasan Ria Ricis Oplas Hidung" (0.358 vs `Inflasi`) — now `saved`, chunked, in both FAISS stores; the same run rejected 177 `ekonomi` rows. Today's extraction is clean (405/405 `site`, avg 3,257 chars, no boilerplate); the *filter* passes them.
  - **The filter, corpus-wide → SYS-31 (#86, P1).** Random 600 saved articles: **53 % score in [0.35, 0.40)**, median 0.396, p90 0.489, none below 0.35 (everything saved passed once). By `sitemaps.category` alone **≥ 700 saved articles (5.7 %)** cannot be finance (TVONENEWS lifestyle/religi/bola 334, MEDIAINDONESIA fashion/forum-mahasiswa/hiburan 115, GRIDID Nakita/Sajian/Nova/… 110, SINDONEWS lifestyle/otomotif/sports/edukasi 89, IDXCHANNEL idxtainment 25, CNN hiburan/gaya-hidup 16, CNBC lifestyle 11) — a floor, PIKIRANRAKYAT's 3,374 regional-portal rows and JPNN's politik/daerah excluded. Verbatim saved titles: *6 Langkah Merebus Telur Agar Mudah Dikupas*, *Ria Ricis Nangis Lihat Sapi Kurbannya Disembelih*, *Ruben Onsu Pernah Coba Temui Anak*, *Presiden Prabowo Pimpin Upacara Hari Lahir Pancasila*, *Cegah Hantavirus, Sudinkes Kepulauan Seribu Gencarkan PHBS*. Controls: rendang recipe 0.174, Liga 1 0.198, skincare 0.222, bridge inauguration 0.090 (rejected) — celebrity-cow-sacrifice **0.421** (passes, above the corpus median). DBT-04 (#23) says these branches are "then discarded by the semantic filter"; they are not.
  - **OPS-15's guard → OPS-16 (#87, P3).** `_record_fetch_failures` is a pure ratio: no floor, no cooldown stamp, nothing persisted. A queue drained to N dead URLs is 100 % every run ⇒ never counted, never cooled, re-fetched every run forever (SYS-09's shape back for the tail; BISNIS is at 3 eligible rows); a dead newest-1,000 window stalls a 10k+ queue; `crawl_log` says `completed/0/NULL`, identical to "all off-topic", and the ERROR goes to stderr only.
  - **`recrawl_faiss_orphans.py` → DBT-27 (#88, P3).** Deletes production L1 vectors; zero tests (the batch's own DBT-19/DBT-25 rule not applied to its own new script); `unrecoverable()` writes off at `fetch_failures > 0`, i.e. after one transient timeout; `--write-off-remaining` absent from OPERATIONS.md.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **"passed_filter=N saved=N" is a funnel count, not a quality claim** — before calling a re-crawl a recovery, look at the `sitemaps.category` split and five titles of what was saved; DBT-20's win was 73 % real. (2) **The 0.35 cosine gate is inside the noise band of this model** — do not tune the threshold, measure precision on a labelled set first (`probe_filter_precision.py`), and prefer a category deny-list / real classifier (the unused `model_inference/` IndoBERT) over a number. (3) **A ratio guard needs a floor** — 3/3 and 1000/1000 are not the same evidence. (4) **A closing comment's "live check" that is a read-only probe is still a REPL check** — for a write-path fix, run the task once for one small source and reconcile the row delta against the probe; it took 4 minutes here. (5) **When a repair script's hypothesis ("the June text was boilerplate") can be checked from its own backup file, check it** — it was true, and it changed the reason the write-off was right.
- **Links:** filed SYS-31 (#86, P1), OPS-16 (#87), DBT-27 (#88) · verification comments on DBT-20 (#70), OPS-15 (#81), SYS-29 (#84) · verified closed: SYS-29, OPS-14, DBT-24, SYS-30, DBT-25, OPS-15, DBT-20 · `scripts/experiments/probe_filter_precision.py` (new, reusable — SYS-31's Done-when check; exit 1 while any control text passes) · `crawl_log` 406 (the CNBC sitemap run) · the mutation harness and DB probes were throwaway scratch, reproduced inline above

### 2026-09-19 — SRC-02..06: what is actually wrong with the 11 BROKEN/ERROR outlets today
- **Question / hypothesis:** the 05:30 alert still names 10 BROKEN + 1 ERROR (TEMPO) every morning. SRC-02..06 were diagnosed on 2026-09-15 from status codes; four days later, is each diagnosis still true, does a working replacement sitemap exist, and what does each fix actually cost?
- **Method:** `scripts/experiments/probe_broken_sources.py` (new, reusable): per outlet the configured URL through the real `RequestsPageLoader.get_soup_with_reason` (OPS-15 reason) + a raw GET, `robots.txt` `Sitemap:` lines, then every candidate (robots + 8 conventional paths) fetched and classified index/leaf/html with counts and first `<loc>`s. Follow-ups by hand (scratch): each candidate finance leaf fetched and checked for `news:publication_date` / `news:title` / `lastmod`; the hardcoded `AntaraNewsNetwork` (60 RSS) and `SuaraNetwork` (20 regional) branch lists fetched; `openssl s_client -showcerts` on inews.id; the venv `certifi` bundle grepped for the missing root and the current `certifi` wheel downloaded and used as `verify=`; each crawler's `_get_branches`/`_scrape` read.
- **Result (per outlet; reasons are the loader's own strings):**
  - **ANTARANEWS** `HTTP 404` — but `AntaraNewsCrawler._get_branches` never reads the index; it returns 60 hardcoded `www.antaranews.com/rss/*.xml` feeds and `_scrape` is RSS-aware. `rss/ekonomi.xml`, `ekonomi-finansial.xml`, `ekonomi-bisnis.xml`, `ekonomi-bursa.xml`: **200, 20 items each, today**. Fix = one line (any 200 URL in the enum), plus restricting the 60 branches to the 5 `ekonomi*` ones (55 are politik/hukum/metro/sepakbola).
  - **SUARA** `URL.SUARA == ""` — same pattern, `SuaraNetwork` returns 20 regional sitemaps that **all return 200 with 150 `<url>` and are all frozen at 2022-10-23**. The live target is `www.suara.com/sitemap.xml` → `bisnis/sitemap-news.xml` (200 `<url>`, `news:` tags, today). Setting the URL alone would ingest ~3,000 four-year-old URLs and look OK.
  - **TIRTO** `HTTP 404` — `tirto.id/sitemap/news.xml` is a 42-section index; `insider/ekonomi.xml` → monthly leaves (272 `<url>` for September) with **`lastmod` only, no title/date tags**. Medium fix (two-level branches + `lastmod` timestamp + a headline source).
  - **BATAMPOS** `HTTP 404` — moved to Yoast `sitemap_index.xml` (152 `post-sitemapN.xml`, `lastmod` only, `post-sitemap.xml` starts in 2016, `post-sitemap151.xml` returned 0 `<url>` on the second fetch). Regional Batam paper, no sections. **Recommend retiring.**
  - **KAPANLAGI** `HTTP 404` — `sitemap/sitemap.xml` is 26 entries of `artis/selebriti/korea/jepang/china/film/foto`; **no economy section exists**. **Recommend retiring** (fixing it only feeds SYS-31).
  - **BISNIS** `HTTP 403` — 5 KB challenge page on every path including robots' own `Sitemap:`. Still blocked; unchanged.
  - **IDNTIMES** `HTTP 403` on `/sitemap-news.xml` **but `/sitemap.xml` is 200** (63-entry index) and `business/sitemap-news.xml` is 200 / 145 `<url>` / `news:` tags / today. Not blocked — wrong path. One line + filter to `sitemap-news.xml` entries. SRC-03's "blocked regardless of UA" is now wrong for IDNTIMES.
  - **INEWS** `SSLError after 5 retries` — chain is `*.inews.id` ← `Sectigo … CA DV R36` ← (missing) `Sectigo Public Server Authentication Root R46`; **`certifi==2022.12.7` has no R46** (0 matches), `certifi 2026.7.22` has it (3) and `verify=<that bundle>` → **200, 36 KB**. Fix = `pip install -U certifi` + pin. Index is 177 sections, `finance/sitemap.xml` is a proper news sitemap.
  - **LIPUTAN6** index 200 / 28 `<section>/sitemap.xml`; `_get_branches` filters on `sitemap_news` → 0. `bisnis/sitemap.xml` = 200 `<url>`, `news:` tags, today. Regex fix, as filed.
  - **MERDEKA** — `_get_branches` returns `{"news": <the index URL>}` without reading the soup, so `_scrape` reads an index → 0 `<url>`. `uang/sitemap.xml` is a textbook news sitemap (100 `<url>`, `news:` tags). Small fix.
  - **TEMPO** — as the 09-18 entry; plus the index itself took **46.9 s** through the loader today (timeout + retry; raw GETs 0.5 s), and `ekonomi-sitemap.xml` is **`lastmod` only, no titles**. Medium, not small.
  - Cross-cutting: `check_crawlers.py` reported `sitemap fetch failed (get_soup returned None)` for 8 of the 10 — it still calls `get_soup`, not `get_soup_with_reason`, and it never reads listing dates, so SUARA's frozen 2022 sitemaps would pass as OK → **OPS-17 (#89)**.
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **Read `_get_branches` before believing "sitemap URL is dead"** — two of the five SRC-02 outlets never parse the index at all; the enum URL is a liveness probe, and the real branch list lives in a `*_branch.py`. (2) **A 200 sitemap with entries can be frozen** — check the newest date, not the count; SUARA's 20 regional sitemaps have been serving October 2022 for four years. (3) **`lastmod`-only sitemaps (TIRTO, TEMPO, BATAMPOS/Yoast) are a different crawler shape** from Google-news sitemaps: no title, no `news:publication_date`; budget a `_scrape` override, not a URL swap. (4) **Retire, don't fix, sources with no finance section** (KAPANLAGI) or no usable sitemap (BATAMPOS) — with SYS-31 open, every off-topic source fixed is pollution added. (5) **A pinned `certifi` is a time bomb** — 2022.12.7 lacks a 2021 Sectigo root; check the bundle before touching `verify=`.

### 2026-09-20 — QA audit of the 2026-09-19/20 remediation batch: does it all actually hold?
- **Question / hypothesis:** the dev session closed ~19 tickets in one sitting (SRC-02, SRC-05, SRC-06, SRC-12..16, SYS-31, SYS-32, DBT-10 ×2, DBT-15, DBT-28, plus docs-only SRC-03/SRC-07/OPS-04) on commits `d572bc8`..`da74a57` (`dev`, unmerged to `main`). Does each fix hold under code review, mutation testing, and a *real* run (not just `check_crawlers.py`'s dry-run sample, which never touches `sitemaps`/`articles`/`chunks`/FAISS)?
- **Method:** full diff read for every commit; `tests/unit` run whole (543 passed); two targeted mutation tests (revert `chunk_faiss_data_source._atomic_save_local`'s backup `copy2`→`move`; revert `importance_filter`'s word-boundary `sport` regex→plain substring) with `git checkout --` restore after each; production DB queried directly (10,772 articles) to recompute `is_offtopic_category` over every saved article and cross-check SYS-31's claimed 1,593-row cleanup; `crawl_log`/`sitemaps` checked for whether any of the 8 newly-fixed outlets had ever produced a real row; **two real production writes:** `task=sitemap` for TIRTO and SUARA (the two outlets with the most novel logic: two-level lastmod traversal + URL-slug headline fallback, and the shared-code `category=None` special-case removal); `check_crawlers.py --website` re-run live for all 11 touched outlets plus BISNIS/KUMPARAN/OKEZONE; `gh issue view`/`gh issue list` to confirm every claimed closure is real (not just `Fixes #N` on a branch that never reaches `main`) and that SRC-03/OPS-04 were correctly left open.
- **Result:**
  - **DB cross-check confirms SYS-31's cleanup exactly:** re-running `is_offtopic_category` over all 10,772 current articles finds **0** that should have been deleted and weren't, and **0** entity-referenced skips — matches the claimed 1,593-delete run with nothing missed and nothing over-deleted.
  - **Both mutations caught.** Reverting the `.bak` step from `copy2` to `move` breaks `TestCrashBetweenTheTwoFileSwaps::test_crash_after_pkl_swap_before_faiss_swap_leaves_a_loadable_stale_pair` (index.faiss goes missing, `RuntimeError` on load) — the "failure-injection test caught a real bug" claim in DBT-10's commit message is backed by a real, committed regression test, not just an interactive finding. Reverting the `sport` word-boundary regex breaks `test_transportasi_is_not_a_false_positive_via_the_sport_substring`.
  - **`check_crawlers.py` re-run live, all 11 outlets: 0 BROKEN/ERROR/timeout among the ones claimed fixed.** ANTARANEWS/LIPUTAN6 `OK`; SUARA/IDNTIMES/MERDEKA/TIRTO/TEMPO/INEWS `FALLBACK` (separately ticketed dead selectors, as each commit said); KUMPARAN/OKEZONE `FALLBACK` (SRC-07, correctly closed); BISNIS still `BROKEN` (403, SRC-03, correctly left open).
  - **Real `task=sitemap` run, TIRTO + SUARA — matches commit claims exactly.** TIRTO: 286 new sitemaps (11 `bisnis` + 275 `ekonomi`), 0 `skipped_no_date` warnings, every row has a non-null `headline` (URL-slug fallback, e.g. `"Pertamina Percepat Produksi Migas Dukung Kemandirian Energi Hc2I"`) and a non-null `posted_at` from `lastmod`. SUARA: 200 new sitemaps, real titles, `category='bisnis'`, dates 2026-09-15..19 (not the old frozen 2022-10-23). This is the first time either fix has been exercised against the real save path — `check_crawlers.py` only samples extraction, it never calls `save_sitemaps`.
  - **Two new, unrelated bugs found and filed** (neither touches any file this batch changed at runtime): **DBT-29 (#107)** — `scripts/backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`'s `classify_batch` still does the pre-SYS-31 raw-threshold check and doesn't even select `sitemaps.category`, so it can no longer reproduce what the live filter actually rejects; a category-denied row with a headline that clears the raw threshold is left `pending` forever instead of `rejected_filter`. **DBT-30 (#108)** — EXPERIMENTS.md's own SRC-07 closure line says "#19" (that's SRC-08); every other mention in the file correctly says "#18".
  - **Everything else read clean:** SRC-02's retirement (KAPANLAGI/BATAMPOS) removed from all four registration points with no dangling references (`CRAWLER_DICT` now imports cleanly, 30 entries); DBT-10's second commit found and fixed all `save_local` call sites in the three repair scripts it named (a repo-wide grep for `.save_local(` after the fact turned up only `chunk_faiss_data_source.py`'s own internal call and one unrelated dead-code path — `faiss_news_data_source.py`'s Ollama-based `FAISSDatasource`, referenced only by the legacy top-level `tests/test_faiss_data_source.py`, never wired into `DataFlowRepositoryImpl` — correctly out of this ticket's scope); DBT-15's double-checked locking is correct (both singleton getters share one lock, covering the real `_db_client` race); every `Fixes #N` in this batch is genuinely `CLOSED` on GitHub despite `dev` not being merged to `main` (closed by explicit `gh issue close`, not autolink — the EXPERIMENTS.md trap about `dev` never auto-closing did not bite here).
- **Conclusion / do not repeat:** (1) **`check_crawlers.py` is a pure sampler — it never calls `save_sitemaps`/`save_newsdetails`/`save_chunk_data`, so "verified with check_crawlers.py" is extraction-only evidence, not save-path evidence.** For a fix that touches sitemap parsing, dedup, or a new DTO-construction fallback (SYS-32's headline/timestamp fallback, SRC-16's two-level traversal), also run one real `task=sitemap` (cheap: no full-text fetch, no embedding) and check the row count and a few columns before calling it done. (2) **A maintenance script that "re-implements the same decision" (`backfill_sitemap_crawl_status.py`, `probe_sys16_backfill_accuracy.py`) is a second copy of the filter's decision boundary that nothing keeps in sync** — grep for other re-implementations whenever `importance_filter.py`'s decision function changes, don't just fix the one call site the ticket named. (3) A repo-wide grep for the exact pattern a fix was supposed to eliminate (`\.save_local\(`) after the fix lands is cheap insurance against "fixed 3 of 4 call sites."
